2026-10-09T04:18:36.0426189Z         """The portability claim, decided by a compiler instead of by a substring.
2026-10-09T04:18:36.0426232Z     
2026-10-09T04:18:36.0426359Z         Strict output must pass `-std=c99 -pedantic-errors`; the default output of the
2026-10-09T04:18:36.0426485Z         same program must *fail* it.  The failing half is the non-vacuity guard: if the
2026-10-09T04:18:36.0426610Z         default were accepted too, "strict mode is portable" would carry no information.
2026-10-09T04:18:36.0426653Z     
2026-10-09T04:18:36.0426765Z         ``-D__extension__=`` is not a trick for its own sake; it is required for the
2026-10-09T04:18:36.0426871Z         guard to mean anything.  GCC's ``__extension__`` keyword exists precisely to
2026-10-09T04:18:36.0426992Z         silence ``-pedantic`` for the construct that follows it, so `-pedantic-errors`
2026-10-09T04:18:36.0427109Z         alone accepts the default bundle *because* every statement expression is
2026-10-09T04:18:36.0427221Z         wrapped in ``__extension__`` (measured: 0 errors with the keyword, 9 without).
2026-10-09T04:18:36.0427345Z         Neutralising the keyword asks the compiler the question that matters: does this
2026-10-09T04:18:36.0427418Z         translation unit rely on a GNU extension?
2026-10-09T04:18:36.0427458Z         """
2026-10-09T04:18:36.0427578Z         strict_exe = compile_c(gen_bundle(_PROG, strict_c99=True), name="strict_prog",
2026-10-09T04:18:36.0427643Z                                std="c99", pedantic=True)
2026-10-09T04:18:36.0427778Z         run = subprocess.run([str(strict_exe)], capture_output=True, text=True, timeout=60)
